Be considerate with her now and ever hot Libertad, for you are all,  
Bend your proud neck to the long-off mother now sending messages  
over the archipelagoes to you,  
Bend your proud neck low for once, young Libertad.  

Here the children straying westward so long? so wide the tramping?  
Were the precedent dim ages debouching westward from Paradise so long?  
Were the centuries steadily footing it that way, all the while  
unknown, for you, for reasons?  

They are justified, they are accomplish’d, they shall now be turn’d  
the other way also, to travel toward you thence,  
They shall now also march obediently eastward for your sake Libertad.  






<span class="qlang-split" data-lang="it"></span>

1  
Sul mare occidentale, giungono da Niphon,  
quei due inviati dal viso scuro e armati di spada;  
appoggiati allo schienale dei loro barocchi aperti, a capo scoperto, impassibili,  
viaggiano oggi attraverso [[settings/manhattan|Manhattan]].  

Libertà! Non so se anche gli altri vedano ciò che vedo io:  
nella processione insieme ai nobili di Niphon, quei portatori di messaggi,  
che seguono in coda, volteggiano sopra, intorno o tra le file in marcia.
Ma ti canterò una canzone di ciò che vedo, Libertà.  

Quando l’immenso [[settings/manhattan|Manhattan]] scende silenziosamente sulle sue strade,  
quando i cannoni rimbombanti mi risvegliano con il possente fragore dell’[[motifs/love|amore]],  
quando quelle bocche rotonde spargono saluti tra il fumo e l’odore della battaglia,  
quando le fiamme dei cannoni mi avvertono pienamente del pericolo,  
e quando le nuvole celesti avvolgono la mia [[motifs/city|città]] in un velo sottile e delicato.
Quando i numerosi alberi lungo i moli si tingono di colori splendidi,  
quando ogni [[motifs/ship|nave]], splendidamente decorata, esibisce la propria [[motifs/flag|bandiera]] in cima,  
quando bandiere sventolano e decorazioni adornano le finestre,  
quando Broadway è piena di pedoni e spettatori, quando la folla è più densa che mai.
Quando le facciate delle case sono piene di persone, quando migliaia di occhi fissano lo stesso punto nello stesso momento.
Quando gli ospiti dalle isole arrivano, quando la processione si avvicina visibilmente.
Quando arriva il segnale, quando risponde quella risposta che aspettava da migliaia di anni.
Anch’io mi alzo, rispondo a quel richiamo e scendo tra la folla, per guardare insieme a loro.  

2  
Superbo [[settings/manhattan|uomo di Manhattan]]!  
Compagni americani. Finalmente il [[settings/orient|Oriente]] arriva da noi.  
A noi, la mia [[motifs/city|città]],  
Dove le nostre imponenti opere in marmo e ferro si ergono su lati opposti,  
Per camminare nello spazio che le separa.
Oggi arriva il nostro “Antipode”.  

La Creatrice arriva;  
Il nido delle lingue, la donatrice di poesie, la razza degli antichi.
Vivace nel suo [[motifs/blood|sangue]], pensierosa, immersa nei suoi ragionamenti, ardente di passione.
Avvolta in abiti ampi e fluttuanti, con il viso abbronzato, un’anima intensa e occhi scintillanti.
Arriva la razza di Brahma.  

Guardate il mio canto! Questi e molti altri elementi ci vengono mostrati dalla processione;  
Mentre si muove e cambia, sembra un kaleidoscopio divino che si trasforma davanti ai nostri occhi.  


Non solo gli inviati né i giapponesi dalla loro isola;  
snello e silenzioso appare l’indiano, appare il continente asiatico stesso,  
il passato, i morti.
La cupa notte-alba dello [[concepts/wonder|meraviglioso]] e dell’[[forms/fable|fable]], incomprensibile.
I misteri avvolti nel velo del tempo, le antiche e sconosciute “api” di questo mondo.
Il nord, il soffocante sud, l’Assiria orientale, gli Ebrei.
Tutti questi, e molto altro ancora, fanno parte di questa processione spettacolare.  

La geografia, il mondo intero, è lì dentro:  
Il Grande Mare, le isole sparse, la Polinesia, le coste che si estendono oltre.
Le coste che ora tu affronti, o Libertà! dalle tue sponde occidentali dorate.
I paesi laggiù, con le loro popolazioni, milioni di persone riunite insieme.
I mercati affollati, i templi con gli idoli disposti lungo i lati o in fondo. Bonzi, brahmani, llama.
Mandarini, contadini, commercianti, meccanici, pescatori.
Le ragazze che cantano e quelle che danzano, le persone estatiche, gli imperatori reclusi.
Lo stesso Confucio, i grandi poeti ed eroi, i guerrieri, tutte le caste.
Tutti quanti, radunandosi da ogni direzione: dalle montagne dell’Altai, dal Tibet, dai quattro fiumi tortuosi e maestosi della Cina, dalle penisole meridionali e dalle isole semicontinentali, dalla Malesia.
Tutto ciò mi appare davanti agli occhi; io lo afferro, ne faccio mio.
E anch’io sono afferrato da esso, tenuto a me con affetto.
Fino a quando, qui, li canto tutti insieme: Libertà! Per loro e per te.  

Poiché anch'io, alzando la voce, mi unisco alle file di questo corteo,  
sono il cantore che proclama ad alta voce le glorie di questo evento;  
canto il mondo sul mio mare occidentale,  
canto le isole lontane, numerose come le [[motifs/stars|stelle]] nel cielo;  
canto un nuovo impero, più grandioso di qualsiasi altro mai esistito, così come mi appare in una visione.
Canto [[settings/america|America]], la padrona del mondo, proclamo una supremazia ancora maggiore;  
canto città fiorite che sorgeranno un giorno su quelle isole marine.
Le mie navi a vela e a vapore solcano gli arcipelaghi;  
le mie bandiere sventolano al [[motifs/wind|vento]]»;  
il commercio si apre, il “sonno” di secoli è finalmente terminato. Le razze rinascono, rinnovate.
Vite e opere riprendono. L’obiettivo, non lo so esattamente. Ma ciò che deve essere rinnovato è il passato, l’Asia.
Tutto inizia da oggi, circondati dal mondo intero.  

3  
E tu, Libertà del mondo!  
Siederai al centro di quel luogo ben collocato per migliaia e migliaia di anni;  
oggi da un lato verranno a te i nobili dell’Asia,  
domani dall’altro lato la [[characters/queen|regina]] d’Inghilterra manderà suo figlio maggiore da te.  

Il cartello si sta invertendo, la sfera è racchiusa dentro;  
l’anello è circondato, il [[motifs/journey|viaggio]] è terminato.
Il coperchio della scatola è appena aperto, eppure il profumo  
si riversa abbondantemente da tutta la scatola.  

Giovane Libertà! Insieme alla venerabile Asia, la madre di tutte,  
sii considerata con lei ora e per sempre, o Libertà. Poiché voi tutti,  
piegate il vostro orgoglioso collo davanti a questa madre lontana che ora vi manda messaggi attraverso gli arcipelaghi.
Piegate il vostro orgoglioso collo, una volta almeno, giovane Libertà.  

Dove sono finiti questi bambini che si sono allontanati verso ovest? Perché hanno percorso strade così lunghe e distanti?  
Forse in passato, in tempi lontani, altri esseri umani si sono diretti verso ovest, partendo dal Paradiso.
O forse, nel corso dei secoli, molte persone hanno continuato a viaggiare in quella direzione, senza che nessuno ne conoscesse il motivo.  

Sono giustificati, sono stati realizzati; ora anche loro saranno rivolti nella direzione opposta, per viaggiare verso di te da lì. Ora anch'essi marceranno obbedientemente verso est, per il tuo bene, Libertà.



<span class="atom-split" data-atom="103_as_i_ebb_d_with_the_ocean_of_life" data-title="As I Ebb’d with the Ocean of Life" data-chapter="" data-kind="intro" data-tags="archetype/great_mother,motif/the_sea,motif/autumn,concept/doubt,concept/self_and_soul,concept/identity,concept/melancholy,form/free_verse,cluster/identity,cluster/free_verse,cluster/eternal_return"></span>


_Cluster: SEA-DRIFT_  


1  
As I ebb’d with the ocean of life,  
As I wended the shores I know,  
As I walk’d where the ripples continually wash you Paumanok,  
Where they rustle up hoarse and sibilant,  
Where the fierce old mother endlessly cries for her castaways,  
I musing late in the [[motifs/autumn|autumn]] day, gazing off southward,  
Held by this electric [[concepts/self|self]] out of the [[concepts/pride|pride]] of which I utter poems,  
Was seiz’d by the spirit that trails in the lines underfoot,  
The rim, the sediment that stands for all the [[motifs/water|water]] and all the land  
of the globe.  

Fascinated, my eyes reverting from the south, dropt, to follow those  
slender windrows,  
Chaff, straw, splinters of wood, weeds, and the sea-gluten,  
Scum, scales from shining rocks, [[motifs/leaves|leaves]] of salt-lettuce, left by the tide,  
Miles walking, the sound of breaking waves the other side of me,  
Paumanok there and then as I thought the old thought of likenesses,  
These you presented to me you fish-shaped island,  
As I wended the shores I know,  
As I walk’d with that electric [[concepts/self|self]] seeking types.  

2  
As I wend to the shores I know not,  
As I list to the [[forms/dirge|dirge]], the voices of men and women wreck’d,  
As I inhale the impalpable breezes that set in upon me,  
As the ocean so mysterious rolls toward me closer and closer,  
I too but signify at the utmost a little wash’d-up drift,  
A few sands and dead [[motifs/leaves|leaves]] to gather,  
Gather, and merge myself as part of the sands and drift.  

O baffled, balk’d, bent to the very earth,  
Oppress’d with myself that I have dared to open my mouth,  
Aware now that amid all that blab whose echoes recoil upon me I have  
not once had the least idea who or what I am,  
But that before all my arrogant poems the real Me stands yet  
untouch’d, untold, altogether unreach’d,  
Withdrawn far, mocking me with mock-congratulatory signs and bows,  
With peals of distant ironical laughter at every word I have written,  
Pointing in [[motifs/silence|silence]] to these songs, and then to the sand beneath.  

I perceive I have not really understood any thing, not a single  
object, and that no man ever can,  
[[concepts/nature|Nature]] here in sight of the sea taking advantage of me to dart upon  
me and sting me,  
Because I have dared to open my mouth to sing at all.  

3  
You oceans both, I close with you,  
We murmur alike reproachfully rolling sands and drift, knowing not why,  
These little shreds indeed standing for you and me and all.  

You friable shore with trails of debris,  
You fish-shaped island, I take what is underfoot,  
What is yours is mine my father.  

I too Paumanok,  
I too have bubbled up, floated the measureless float, and been  
wash’d on your shores,  
I too am but a trail of drift and debris,  
I too leave little wrecks upon you, you fish-shaped island.  

I throw myself upon your breast my father,  
I cling to you so that you cannot unloose me,  
I hold you so firm till you answer me something.  

Kiss me my father,  
Touch me with your lips as I touch those I [[motifs/love|love]],  
Breathe to me while I hold you close the secret of the murmuring I envy.  

4  
Ebb, ocean of life, (the flow will return,)  
Cease not your moaning you fierce old mother,  
Endlessly cry for your castaways, but [[concepts/fear|fear]] not, deny not me,  
Rustle not up so hoarse and angry against my feet as I touch you or  
gather from you.  

I mean tenderly by you and all,  
I gather for myself and for this [[characters/phantom|phantom]] looking down where we lead,  
and following me and mine.  

Me and mine, loose windrows, little corpses,  
Froth, snowy white, and bubbles,  
(See, from my dead lips the ooze exuding at last,  
See, the prismatic colors glistening and rolling,)  
Tufts of straw, sands, fragments,  
Buoy’d hither from many moods, one contradicting another,  
From the [[motifs/storm|storm]], the long calm, the darkness, the swell,  
Musing, pondering, a breath, a briny tear, a dab of liquid or soil,  
Up just as much out of fathomless workings fermented and thrown,  
A limp blossom or two, torn, just as much over waves floating,  
drifted at random,  
Just as much for us that sobbing [[forms/dirge|dirge]] of [[concepts/nature|Nature]],  
Just as much whence we come that blare of the cloud-trumpets,  
We, capricious, brought hither we know not whence, spread out before you,  
You up there walking or sitting,  
Whoever you are, we too lie in drifts at your feet.  






<span class="qlang-split" data-lang="it"></span>

_Cluster: SEA-DRIFT_  


1  
Mentre mi perdevo nell’oceano della vita,  
mentre costeggiavo le rive che conoscevo bene,  
mentre camminavo dove le increspature continuavano a bagnare Paumanok,  
dove il suono delle onde era roco e sibilante,  
dove la “fiera madre” del mare piangeva senza fine per i suoi naufraghi.
Meditando tardi, in una giornata d’[[motifs/autumn|autunno]], guardavo verso sud;  
trattenuto da questo spirito interiore che mi guidava,  
lasciandomi libero dall’[[concepts/pride|orgoglio]] dal quale nascevano i miei poemi.
Fui colpito da quel sentimento che si nasconde nelle linee lasciate dalle onde sulla riva,  
da quei sedimenti che rappresentano tutta l’[[motifs/water|acqua]] e tutta la terra  
di questo pianeta.  

Fascinato, i miei occhi abbandonarono il sud e si abbassarono per seguire quelle sottili file di detriti: paglia, schegge di legno, erbacce. E poi la schiuma del mare, le scaglie delle rocce lucide, le [[motifs/leaves|foglie]] di lattuga salata lasciate dalla marea. Camminai per miglia; il suono delle onde che si frantumavano mi risuonava alle spalle. A un certo punto, mentre ripensavo a quell’antica idea di somiglianze, eccoti qui, isola a forma di pesce che mi hai mostrato. Mentre percorrevo le tue rive, con quel “io” elettrico che cercava sempre nuovi modelli da osservare.  

2  
Mentre mi dirigo verso coste sconosciute,  
ascolto le voci di uomini e donne distrutti dalla tragedia;  
respiro brezze impercettibili che mi avvolgono;  
l’oceano misterioso si avvicina sempre di più.
Anch’io non sono altro che un piccolo frammento trascinato via dalle onde,  
alcune sabbie e foglie secche da raccogliere.
Raccoglierle, e fondermi con esse, diventando parte di quel tutto.  

O confuso, esitante, piegato fino a toccare la terra.
Oppresso da me stesso per aver osato aprire bocca.
Ora so che, in mezzo a tutto quel vaniloquio il cui eco mi ritorna addosso,  
Non ho mai avuto la minima idea di chi o cosa io sia.
Ma che, davanti a tutti i miei arroganti poemi, il vero Me rimane ancora  
Intatto, inespresso, del tutto irraggiungibile.
Ritirato lontano, che si burla di me con gesti di falso complimento e inchini.
Con risate ironiche e distanti a ogni parola che scrivo.
Che indica, nel [[motifs/silence|silenzio]], queste cantate. E poi la sabbia sotto i miei piedi.  

Percepisco di non aver davvero capito nulla, nemmeno un singolo concetto; e che nessun uomo possa mai farlo. La [[concepts/nature|Natura]], qui, davanti al mare, approfitta della mia presenza per attaccarmi e pungermi, perché ho osato aprire bocca e cantare.  

3  
Oceani voi due, con voi mi concludo;  
Mormoriamo entrambi, come sabbie che si muovono e correnti che scorrono, in tono di rimprovero, senza sapere il perché.
Questi piccoli frammenti, in realtà, rappresentano te, me e tutti quanti.  

Tu, fragile riva con i sentieri di detriti,  
tu, isola a forma di pesce. Prendo ciò che ho sotto i piedi;  
ciò che è tuo è mio. Mio padre.  

Anch'io, Paumanok,  
anch'io sono emerso dalle profondità, ho galleggiato su quel mare infinito e sono stato portato sulle tue rive;  
anch'io non sono altro che un filo di detriti e resti,  
anch'io lascio dietro di me poche tracce sulla tua isola a forma di pesce.  

Mi getto sul tuo petto, mio padre,  
mi aggrappo a te così forte che non puoi liberarmi,  
ti tengo stretto fino a quando non mi risponderai qualcosa.  

Baciami, mio padre.
Toccami con le tue labbra, proprio come io tocco coloro che amo.
Respira per me mentre ti tengo stretto. Il segreto di quel sussurro che invidio.  

4  
Ebb, oceano di vita, (il flusso tornerà),  
Non smettere mai il tuo lamento, tu madre terribile e antica,  
Piangi senza fine per i tuoi naufraghi, ma non [[concepts/fear|temere]], non negarmi,  
Non far rumore così forte e arrabbiato quando io ti tocco o raccolgo da te.  

Voglio dire, con tenerezza, riguardo a te e a tutti.
Raccolgo per me stesso, e anche per questo [[characters/phantom|fantasma]] che osserva da lassù dove ci dirigiamo,  
e che segue me e i miei passi.  

Io e i miei simili, mucchi disordinati, piccoli “corpi senza vita”,  
schiuma, bianca come la neve, bolle.
(Vedi: dalle mie labbra morte finalmente sgorga questo liquido vischioso;  
vedi i colori prismatici che brillano e si muovono, )  
Mazzi di paglia, sabbia, frammenti.
Trasportati qui da mille emozioni contrastanti: la tempesta, la lunga calma, l’oscurità.
Pensieri, riflessioni. Un respiro, una lacrima salata, un goccio di liquido o di terra.
Tutto questo emerge da processi incomprensibili, fermentati e gettati via.
Un fiore appassito, strappato. Che galleggia sulle onde, disperso a caso.
Per noi, come per quella “lamentela” che la Natura emette.
Come per quel suono fragoroso prodotto dalle trombe delle nuvole.
Noi, capricciosi, siamo stati portati qui da chissà dove. Sparsi davanti a te.
Tu, lassù, che cammini o stai seduto. Chiunque tu sia. Anche noi giacciamo ai tuoi piedi, dispersi.



<span class="atom-split" data-atom="104_tears" data-title="Tears" data-chapter="" data-kind="intro" data-tags="motif/night,motif/storm,motif/the_sea,concept/grief_and_loss,concept/melancholy,form/free_verse,form/lyric,cluster/identity,cluster/free_verse,cluster/eternal_return"></span>


_Cluster: SEA-DRIFT_  


[[motifs/tears|Tears]]! [[motifs/tears|tears]]! [[motifs/tears|tears]]!  
In the [[motifs/night|night]], in [[concepts/solitude|solitude]], [[motifs/tears|tears]],  
On the white shore dripping, dripping, suck’d in by the sand,  
[[motifs/tears|Tears]], not a star shining, all dark and desolate,  
Moist [[motifs/tears|tears]] from the eyes of a muffled head;  
O who is that [[archetypes/ghost|ghost]]? that form in the dark, with [[motifs/tears|tears]]?  
What shapeless lump is that, bent, crouch’d there on the sand?  
Streaming [[motifs/tears|tears]], sobbing [[motifs/tears|tears]], throes, choked with wild cries;  
O [[motifs/storm|storm]], embodied, rising, careering with swift steps along the beach!  
O wild and dismal [[motifs/night|night]] [[motifs/storm|storm]], with [[motifs/wind|wind]]--O belching and desperate!  
O shade so sedate and decorous by day, with calm countenance and  
regulated pace,  
But away at [[motifs/night|night]] as you fly, none looking--O then the unloosen’d ocean,  
Of [[motifs/tears|tears]]! [[motifs/tears|tears]]! [[motifs/tears|tears]]!  






<span class="qlang-split" data-lang="it"></span>

_Cluster: SEA-DRIFT_  


[[motifs/tears|)Lacrime]]! [[motifs/tears|Lacrime]]! [[motifs/tears|)Lacrime]]!  
Nella [[motifs/night|notte]], nella [[concepts/solitude|solitudine]], quelle lacrime,  
sul bianco bagnato della riva, gocciolano, assorbite dalla sabbia;  
[[motifs/tears|Lacrime]]. Nessuna stella splende; tutto è buio e desolato.  
Lacrime umide scivolano dagli occhi di una testa nascosta.
Oh, chi è quell’[[archetypes/ghost|fantasma]]? Quella figura nell’oscurità, con quelle lacrime.
Che forma senza contorni è quella, curva, accovacciata sulla sabbia.
Lacrime che scorrono, singhiozzi che si susseguono. Gemiti soffocati da grida disperate.
Oh, [[motifs/storm|tempesta]] incarnata, che si alza e corre lungo la riva con passi veloci!  
Oh, notte selvaggia e cupa, tempesta furiosa, con quel vento. Oh, tu che soffi con disperazione!  
Oh, ombra così tranquilla e dignitosa di giorno, con un volto sereno e un passo regolare.
Ma di notte, quando voli via. Nessuno ti vede. Allora l’oceano si apre, pieno di lacrime.
[[motifs/tears|)Lacrime]]! [[motifs/tears|)Lacrime]]! [[motifs/tears|)Lacrime]]!



<span class="atom-split" data-atom="115_a_hand_mirror" data-title="A Hand-Mirror" data-chapter="" data-kind="intro" data-tags="topos/memento_mori,motif/the_mirror,motif/decay,concept/the_body,concept/self_and_soul,form/free_verse,cluster/identity,cluster/free_verse,cluster/eternal_return"></span>


_Cluster: BY THE ROADSIDE_  


Hold it up sternly--see this it sends back, (who is it? is it you?)  
Outside fair costume, within ashes and filth,  
No more a flashing eye, no more a sonorous voice or springy step,  
Now some slave’s eye, voice, hands, step,  
A drunkard’s breath, unwholesome eater’s face, venerealee’s flesh,  
Lungs rotting away piecemeal, stomach sour and cankerous,  
Joints rheumatic, bowels clogged with abomination,  
[[motifs/blood|Blood]] circulating dark and poisonous streams,  
Words babble, hearing and touch callous,  
No brain, no heart left, no magnetism of sex;  
Such from one look in this looking-glass ere you go hence,  
Such a result so soon--and from such a beginning!  






<span class="qlang-split" data-lang="it"></span>

_Cluster: Lungo i bordi delle strade_  


Tienilo ben fermo davanti a te. Vedi? Lo restituisce indietro, (Chi è? Sei tu?)  
All’esterno un bell’abito; dentro cenere e sporcizia.
Nessun occhio scintillante, nessuna voce sonora, nessun passo agile.
Ora sono gli occhi, la voce, le mani, i passi di uno schiavo.
Il respiro di un ubriaco, il viso di chi mangia cibo nocivo, la carne di una prostituta.
Polmoni che marciscono pezzo dopo pezzo, stomaco acido e pieno di cancrena.
Articolazioni reumatiche, intestini intasati da orrori.
[[motifs/blood|Sangue]] che scorre in flussi scuri e velenosi.
Parole balbettate, udito e tatto insensibili.
Nessun cervello, nessun cuore. Nessuna attrattiva sessuale.
Ecco ciò che si vede guardandosi in questo specchio.
Un risultato così rapido. E tutto a partire da un semplice sguardo.



<span class="atom-split" data-atom="118_thoughts" data-title="Thoughts" data-chapter="" data-kind="intro" data-tags="topos/the_open_road,motif/the_journey,concept/the_self,concept/critique_of_progress,form/free_verse,form/epigram,cluster/identity,cluster/free_verse,cluster/eternal_return"></span>


_Cluster: BY THE ROADSIDE_  


Of ownership--as if one fit to own things could not at pleasure enter  
upon all, and incorporate them into himself or herself;  
Of vista--suppose some sight in arriere through the formative chaos,  
presuming the growth, fulness, life, now attain’d on the [[motifs/journey|journey]],  
(But I see the [[motifs/road|road]] continued, and the [[motifs/journey|journey]] ever continued;)  
Of what was once lacking on earth, and in due [[motifs/time|time]] has become  
supplied--and of what will yet be supplied,  
Because all I see and know I believe to have its main purport in  
what will yet be supplied.  





<span class="qlang-split" data-lang="it"></span>

_Cluster: Lungo i bordi delle strade_  


Del possesso: come se colui che è adatto a possedere le cose non potesse, se lo desiderasse, entrare in possesso di tutto e incorporarlo in sé stesso;  
Della visione: supponiamo che esista una visione di ciò che sarà in futuro, attraverso il caos formativo attuale, partendo dal presupposto che la crescita, la pienezza e la vita che si sono ora realizzate nel corso del [[motifs/journey|viaggio]] continueranno.
Di ciò che un tempo mancava sulla terra e che, con il giusto [[motifs/time|tempo]], sarà fornito; e di ciò che ancora verrà fornito.  
Perché tutto ciò che vedo e so sembra avere il suo scopo principale proprio in ciò che ancora verrà fornito.



<span class="atom-split" data-atom="121_o_me_o_life" data-title="O Me! O Life!" data-chapter="" data-kind="intro" data-tags="topos/immortality_through_verse,concept/melancholy,concept/identity,concept/the_self,concept/the_artist_and_society,form/free_verse,cluster/identity,cluster/free_verse,cluster/eternal_return"></span>


_Cluster: BY THE ROADSIDE_  


O me! O life! of the questions of these recurring,  
Of the endless trains of the faithless, of cities fill’d with the foolish,  
Of myself forever reproaching myself, (for who more foolish than I,  
and who more faithless?)  
Of eyes that vainly crave the light, of the objects mean, of the  
struggle ever renew’d,  
Of the poor results of all, of the plodding and sordid crowds I see  
around me,  
Of the empty and useless years of the rest, with the rest me intertwined,  
The question, O me! so sad, recurring--What good amid these, O me, O life?  

Answer.  
That you are here--that life exists and [[concepts/identity|identity]],  
That the powerful play goes on, and you may contribute a verse.  






<span class="qlang-split" data-lang="it"></span>

_Cluster: Lungo i bordi delle strade_  


Oh me! Oh vita. Di tutte queste domande che continuano a ripresentarsi,  
di questi interminabili flussi di persone infide, di città piene di sciocchi,  
di me stesso che continuo a rimproverarmi, (Chi mai è più sciocco di me,  
chi mai è più infido di me?)  
Di occhi che invano anelano alla luce, di oggetti banali, di lotte che si ripetono senza fine.
Dei miseri risultati di tutto ciò, delle folle laboriose e meschine che vedo intorno a me.
Degli anni vuoti e inutili della mia vita. Con tutti gli altri, intrecciato con loro.
La domanda, oh me, così triste, continua a ripresentarsi: Qual è il senso di tutto questo, oh me, oh vita?  

Risposta:  
Il fatto che tu sia qui, il fatto che la vita esista e che ci sia una [[concepts/identity|identità]].
Il fatto che il grande dramma della vita continui, e che anche tu possa contribuire con la tua parte in esso.



<span class="atom-split" data-atom="122_to_a_president" data-title="To a President" data-chapter="" data-kind="intro" data-tags="concept/the_natural_order,concept/reform,concept/democracy,concept/power_and_corruption,form/free_verse,form/epigram,cluster/identity,cluster/free_verse,cluster/eternal_return"></span>


_Cluster: BY THE ROADSIDE_  


All you are doing and saying is to [[settings/america|America]] dangled mirages,  
You have not learn’d of [[concepts/nature|Nature]]--of the politics of [[concepts/nature|Nature]] you have  
not learn’d the great amplitude, rectitude, impartiality,  
You have not seen that only such as they are for these States,  
And that what is less than they must sooner or later lift off from  
these States.  






<span class="qlang-split" data-lang="it"></span>

_Cluster: Lungo i bordi delle strade_  


Tutto ciò che fate e dite non è altro che offrire all’[[settings/america|America]] illusioni; non avete imparato nulla sull’[[concepts/nature|Natura]]. Non avete compreso la politica dell’[[concepts/nature|Natura]], né la sua vastità, rettitudine e imparzialità. Non avete visto che soltanto ciò che è veramente adatto a questi Stati può rimanervi; al contrario, tutto ciò che è inferiore a tali standard dovrà prima o poi essere eliminato da essi.



<span class="atom-split" data-atom="123_i_sit_and_look_out" data-title="I Sit and Look Out" data-chapter="" data-kind="intro" data-tags="motif/eyes_sight,motif/silence,concept/social_injustice,concept/grief_and_loss,concept/power_and_corruption,form/free_verse,cluster/identity,cluster/free_verse,cluster/eternal_return"></span>


_Cluster: BY THE ROADSIDE_  


I sit and look out upon all the sorrows of the world, and upon all  
oppression and shame,  
I hear secret convulsive sobs from young men at anguish with  
themselves, remorseful after deeds done,  
I see in low life the mother misused by her children, dying,  
neglected, gaunt, desperate,  
I see the wife misused by her husband, I see the treacherous seducer  
of young women,  
I mark the ranklings of [[concepts/jealousy|jealousy]] and unrequited [[motifs/love|love]] attempted to be  
hid, I see these sights on the earth,  
I see the workings of battle, pestilence, tyranny, I see martyrs and  
prisoners,  
I observe a famine at sea, I observe the sailors casting lots who  
shall be kill’d to preserve the lives of the rest,  
I observe the slights and degradations cast by arrogant persons upon  
laborers, the poor, and upon negroes, and the like;  
All these--all the meanness and agony without end I sitting look out upon,  
See, hear, and am silent.  






<span class="qlang-split" data-lang="it"></span>

_Cluster: Lungo i bordi delle strade_  


Siedo e osservo tutte le sofferenze del mondo, tutta l’oppressione e la vergogna. Ascolto i sospiri segreti e convulsi di giovani tormentati dai rimorsi per le azioni compiute. Vedo nelle classi più basse madri maltrattate dai propri figli, che muoiono abbandonate, emaciate, disperate. Vedo mogli maltrattate dai mariti, vedo i seduttori traditori delle giovani donne. Noto le ferite causate dalla gelosia e dall’amore non corrisposto, tentate di essere nascoste. Vedo tutto ciò sulla terra: le battaglie, le pestilenze, la tirannia. Martiri e prigionieri. Una carestia in mare. Marinai che lanciano i dadi per decidere chi dovrà morire al fine di salvare gli altri. Insulti e umiliazioni inflitti da persone arroganti ai lavoratori, ai poveri, ai neri. Tutto questo. Tutta questa meschinità e sofferenza senza fine. Io li osservo. Li vedo. Li ascolto. E resto in silenzio.



<span class="atom-split" data-atom="129_the_runner" data-title="The Runner" data-chapter="" data-kind="intro" data-tags="motif/the_road,concept/the_body,form/free_verse,form/fragment,cluster/identity,cluster/free_verse,cluster/eternal_return"></span>


_Cluster: BY THE ROADSIDE_  


On a flat [[motifs/road|road]] runs the [[motifs/well|well]]-train’d runner,  
He is lean and sinewy with muscular legs,  
He is thinly clothed, he leans forward as he runs,  
With lightly closed fists and arms partially rais’d.  






<span class="qlang-split" data-lang="it"></span>

_Cluster: Lungo i bordi delle strade_  


Su una pianeggiante [[motifs/road|strada]] corre il corridore ben allenato;  
è snello e muscoloso, con gambe forti;  
indossa pochi vestiti e si piega in avanti mentre corre,  
con i pugni leggermente chiusi e le braccia sollevate parzialmente.



<span class="atom-split" data-atom="146_song_of_the_banner_at_daybreak" data-title="Song of the Banner at Daybreak" data-chapter="" data-kind="intro" data-tags="topos/poet_as_prophet,archetype/child,motif/flags_banners,motif/dawn,motif/money_gold,motif/drums_and_bugles,concept/war_and_its_cost,concept/nationalism_and_patriotism,form/dramatic_monologue,cluster/identity,cluster/free_verse,cluster/eternal_return"></span>


_Cluster: DRUM-TAPS_  


Poet:  
O A new song, a free song,  
Flapping, flapping, flapping, flapping, by sounds, by voices clearer,  
By the [[motifs/wind|wind]]’s voice and that of the drum,  
By the banner’s voice and [[archetypes/child|child]]’s voice and sea’s voice and father’s voice,  
Low on the ground and high in the air,  
On the ground where father and [[archetypes/child|child]] stand,  
In the upward air where their eyes turn,  
Where the banner at daybreak is flapping.  

Words! book-words! what are you?  
Words no more, for hearken and see,  
My song is there in the open air, and I must sing,  
With the banner and pennant a-flapping.  

I’ll weave the chord and twine in,  
Man’s [[motifs/desire|desire]] and babe’s [[motifs/desire|desire]], I’ll twine them in, I’ll put in life,  
I’ll put the bayonet’s flashing point, I’ll let bullets and slugs whizz,  
(As one carrying a symbol and menace far into the future,  
Crying with trumpet voice, Arouse and beware! Beware and arouse!)  
I’ll pour the verse with streams of [[motifs/blood|blood]], full of volition, full of joy,  
Then loosen, launch forth, to go and compete,  
With the banner and pennant a-flapping.  

Pennant:  
Come up here, bard, bard,  
Come up here, [[motifs/soul|soul]], [[motifs/soul|soul]],  
Come up here, dear little [[archetypes/child|child]],  
To fly in the clouds and winds with me, and play with the measureless light.  

[[archetypes/child|Child]]:  
Father what is that in the sky beckoning to me with long finger?  
And what does it say to me all the while?  

Father:  
Nothing my babe you see in the sky,  
And nothing at all to you it says--but look you my babe,  
Look at these dazzling things in the houses, and see you the money-  
shops opening,  
And see you the vehicles preparing to crawl along the streets with goods;  
These, ah these, how valued and toil’d for these!  
How envied by all the earth.  

Poet:  
Fresh and rosy red the sun is mounting high,  
On floats the sea in distant blue careering through its channels,  
On floats the [[motifs/wind|wind]] over the breast of the sea setting in toward land,  
The great steady [[motifs/wind|wind]] from west or west-by-south,  
Floating so buoyant with milk-white foam on the waters.  

But I am not the sea nor the red sun,  
I am not the [[motifs/wind|wind]] with girlish laughter,  
Not the immense [[motifs/wind|wind]] which strengthens, not the [[motifs/wind|wind]] which lashes,  
Not the spirit that ever lashes its own [[concepts/body|body]] to terror and [[motifs/death|death]],  
But I am that which unseen comes and sings, sings, sings,  
Which babbles in brooks and scoots in showers on the land,  
Which the [[motifs/birds|birds]] know in the woods mornings and evenings,  
And the shore-sands know and the hissing wave, and that banner and pennant,  
Aloft there flapping and flapping.  

[[archetypes/child|Child]]:  
O father it is alive--it is full of people--it has children,  
O now it seems to me it is talking to its children,  
I hear it--it talks to me--O it is wonderful!  
O it stretches--it spreads and runs so fast--O my father,  
It is so broad it covers the whole sky.  

Father:  
Cease, cease, my foolish babe,  
What you are saying is sorrowful to me, much ’t displeases me;  
Behold with the rest again I say, behold not banners and pennants aloft,  
But the [[motifs/well|well]]-prepared pavements behold, and mark the solid-wall’d houses.  

Banner and Pennant:  
Speak to the [[archetypes/child|child]] O bard out of [[settings/manhattan|Manhattan]],  
To our children all, or north or south of [[settings/manhattan|Manhattan]],  
Point this day, leaving all the rest, to us over all--and yet we know  
not why,  
For what are we, mere strips of cloth profiting nothing,  
Only flapping in the [[motifs/wind|wind]]?  


Poet:  
I hear and see not strips of cloth alone,  
I hear the tramp of armies, I hear the challenging sentry,  
I hear the jubilant shouts of millions of men, I hear Liberty!  
I hear the drums beat and the trumpets blowing,  
I myself move abroad swift-rising flying then,  
I use the wings of the land-bird and use the wings of the sea-bird,  
and look down as from a height,  
I do not deny the precious results of peace, I see populous cities  
with wealth incalculable,  
I see numberless farms, I see the farmers working in their fields or barns,  
I see mechanics working, I see buildings everywhere founded, going  
up, or finish’d,  
I see trains of cars swiftly speeding along railroad tracks drawn by  
the locomotives,  
I see the stores, depots, of Boston, Baltimore, Charleston, New Orleans,  
I see far in the West the immense area of grain, I dwell awhile hovering,  
I pass to the lumber forests of the North, and again to the Southern  
plantation, and again to California;  
Sweeping the whole I see the countless profit, the busy gatherings,  
earn’d wages,  
See the [[concepts/identity|Identity]] formed out of thirty-eight spacious and haughty  
States, (and many more to come,)  
See forts on the shores of harbors, see ships sailing in and out;  
Then over all, (aye! aye!) my little and lengthen’d pennant shaped  
like a sword,  
Runs swiftly up indicating war and defiance--and now the halyards  
have rais’d it,  
Side of my banner broad and blue, side of my starry banner,  
Discarding peace over all the sea and land.  

Banner and Pennant:  
Yet louder, higher, stronger, bard! yet farther, wider cleave!  
No longer let our children deem us riches and peace alone,  
We may be terror and carnage, and are so now,  
Not now are we any one of these spacious and haughty States, (nor  
any five, nor ten,)  
Nor market nor depot we, nor money-bank in the [[motifs/city|city]],  
But these and all, and the brown and spreading land, and the mines  
below, are ours,  
And the shores of the sea are ours, and the rivers great and small,  
And the fields they moisten, and the crops and the fruits are ours,  
Bays and channels and ships sailing in and out are ours--while we over all,  
Over the area spread below, the three or four millions of square  
miles, the capitals,  
The forty millions of people,--O bard! in life and [[motifs/death|death]] supreme,  
We, even we, henceforth flaunt out masterful, high up above,  
Not for the present alone, for a thousand years chanting through you,  
This song to the [[motifs/soul|soul]] of one poor little [[archetypes/child|child]].  

[[archetypes/child|Child]]:  
O my father I like not the houses,  
They will never to me be any thing, nor do I like money,  
But to mount up there I would like, O father dear, that banner I like,  
That pennant I would be and must be.  

Father:  
[[archetypes/child|Child]] of mine you fill me with anguish,  
To be that pennant would be too fearful,  
Little you know what it is this day, and after this day, forever,  
It is to gain nothing, but risk and defy every thing,  
Forward to stand in front of wars--and O, such wars!--what have you  
to do with them?  
With passions of demons, slaughter, premature [[motifs/death|death]]?  

Banner:  
Demons and [[motifs/death|death]] then I sing,  
Put in all, aye all will I, sword-shaped pennant for war,  
And a pleasure new and ecstatic, and the prattled yearning of children,  
Blent with the sounds of the peaceful land and the liquid wash of the sea,  
And the black ships fighting on the sea envelop’d in smoke,  
And the icy cool of the far, far north, with rustling cedars and pines,  
And the whirr of drums and the sound of soldiers marching, and the  
hot sun shining south,  
And the beach-waves combing over the beach on my Eastern shore,  
and my Western shore the same,  
And all between those shores, and my ever running Mississippi with  
bends and chutes,  
And my Illinois fields, and my Kansas fields, and my fields of Missouri,  
The Continent, devoting the whole [[concepts/identity|identity]] without reserving an atom,  
Pour in! whelm that which asks, which sings, with all and the yield of all,  
Fusing and holding, claiming, devouring the whole,  
No more with tender lip, nor musical labial sound,  
But out of the [[motifs/night|night]] emerging for good, our voice persuasive no more,  
Croaking like crows here in the [[motifs/wind|wind]].  

Poet:  
My limbs, my veins dilate, my theme is clear at last,  
Banner so broad advancing out of the [[motifs/night|night]], I sing you haughty and resolute,  
I burst through where I waited long, too long, deafen’d and blinded,  
My hearing and tongue are come to me, (a little [[archetypes/child|child]] taught me,)  
I hear from above O pennant of war your ironical call and demand,  
Insensate! insensate! (yet I at any rate chant you,) O banner!  
Not houses of peace indeed are you, nor any nor all their  
prosperity, (if need be, you shall again have every one of those  
houses to destroy them,  
You thought not to destroy those valuable houses, standing fast,  
full of comfort, built with money,  
May they stand fast, then? not an hour except you above them and all  
stand fast;)  
O banner, not money so precious are you, not farm produce you, nor  
the material good nutriment,  
Nor excellent stores, nor landed on wharves from the ships,  
Not the superb ships with sail-power or steam-power, fetching and  
carrying cargoes,  
Nor machinery, vehicles, trade, nor revenues--but you as henceforth  
I see you,  
Running up out of the [[motifs/night|night]], bringing your cluster of [[motifs/stars|stars]],  
(ever-enlarging [[motifs/stars|stars]],)  
Divider of daybreak you, cutting the air, touch’d by the sun,  
measuring the sky,  
(Passionately seen and yearn’d for by one poor little [[archetypes/child|child]],  
While others remain busy or smartly talking, forever teaching  
thrift, thrift;)  
O you up there! O pennant! where you undulate like a snake hissing  
so curious,  
Out of reach, an idea only, yet furiously fought for, risking bloody  
[[motifs/death|death]], loved by me,  
So loved--O you banner leading the day with [[motifs/stars|stars]] brought from the [[motifs/night|night]]!  
Valueless, object of eyes, over all and demanding all--(absolute  
owner of all)--O banner and pennant!  
I too leave the rest--great as it is, it is nothing--houses, machines  
are nothing--I see them not,  
I see but you, O warlike pennant! O banner so broad, with stripes,  
sing you only,  
Flapping up there in the [[motifs/wind|wind]].  






<span class="qlang-split" data-lang="it"></span>

_Cluster: DRUM-TAPS_  


Poeta:  
O una nuova canzone, una canzone libera e senza vincoli.
Che si muove al ritmo dei suoni, delle voci più chiare.
Al suono del [[motifs/wind|vento]] e della batteria.
Al suono della bandiera, della voce di un bambino, del mare, di un padre.
In basso a terra, in alto nel cielo.
Sul terreno dove padre e figlio stanno.
Nel cielo dove i loro sguardi si alzano.
Là dove la bandiera sventola all’alba.  

Parole, parole di un libro, che cosa siete realmente?  
Non sono più semplicemente parole: ascoltate e guardate.
La mia canzone è lì, all’aperto, e devo cantare,  
Con la bandiera e le insegne che sventolano al vento.  

Intreccerò questa corda, avvolgendovi dentro  
il desiderio dell’uomo e quello del bambino; li intreccerò insieme, darò loro vita.
Aggiungerò il bagliore della baionetta, farò sibilare proiettili e pallottole.
(Come colui che porta un simbolo di minaccia nel futuro lontano,  
gridando con voce di tromba: “Svegliatevi! State in guardia!”)  
Ricorderò questi versi con flussi di [[motifs/blood|sangue]], pieni di volontà e gioia.
Poi li lascerò andare, liberi di competere,  
con la bandiera e le insegne che sventolano al vento.  

Pennant:  
Vieni su qui, bardo, bardo,  
Vieni su qui, anima mia, anima mia,  
Vieni su qui, caro piccolo bambino,  
Per volare tra le nuvole e i venti con me, e giocare con quella luce infinita.  

[[archetypes/child|Bambino]]:  
“Padre, cos'è quella cosa nel cielo che mi fa segno con quel dito lungo?  
E perché continua a dire qualcosa a me?”  

Padre:  
Niente, piccola mia. Quello che vedi nel cielo non significa nulla per te. Ma guarda, piccola mia: guarda queste cose splendide nelle case, guarda i negozi che aprono, guarda i veicoli pronti a portare merci per le strade. Queste cose, quanto sono preziose e per cui si lavora sodo! Quanto sono invidiate da tutti.  

Poeta:  
Il sole, fresco e di un rosso vivace, si alza alto nel cielo;  
Il mare, di un azzurro lontano, scorre lungo i suoi canali;  
Il vento soffia sulle onde, dirigendosi verso la terra;  
Il grande, costante vento proveniente da ovest.
Tutto fluttua, portando con sé schiuma bianca come il latte sull’acqua.  

Ma non sono il mare né il sole rosso,  
non sono il [[motifs/wind|vento]] che porta risate giovanili,  
né il vasto [[motifs/wind|vento]] che rafforza, né quello che colpisce con violenza,  
né lo spirito che tormenta il proprio [[concepts/body|-corpo]] con terrore e [[motifs/death|morte]],  
ma sono ciò che, invisibile, arriva e canta, canta senza sosta,  
ciò che mormora nei ruscelli e scorre nelle piogge sulla terra,  
ciò che gli [[motifs/birds|uccelli]] conoscono nelle foreste al mattino e alla sera,  
e che le spiagge, le onde sibilanti, quelle bandiere e quegli stendardi conoscono anch’essi,  
là in alto, che si agitano al vento.  

[[archetypes/child|Bambino]]:  
“Oh padre, è vivo, è pieno di persone, ha anche dei bambini.
Oh, ora mi sembra che stia parlando con i suoi figli.
Lo sento, mi parla. Oh, è meraviglioso!  
Si estende, si allarga e corre così velocemente. Oh mio padre,  
È così vasto da coprire tutto il cielo.”  

Padre:  
Basta, basta, mio sciocco bambino.
Quello che dici mi rattrista molto e mi dispiace profondamente;  
Guarda ancora intorno a te: non osserva le bandiere e i stendardi sventolanti in alto,  
ma guarda invece i marciapiedi ben preparati e le case con muri solidi.  

Stendardo e Bandiera:  
Parla al [[archetypes/child|bambino]], o bardo proveniente da [[settings/manhattan|Manhattan]],  
A tutti i nostri figli, sia a nord che a sud di [[settings/manhattan|Manhattan]];  
Indica questo giorno, lasciando da parte tutto il resto, come il nostro simbolo. Eppure non sappiamo perché;  
Che cosa siamo noi, se non semplici strisce di stoffa che non portano alcun beneficio,  
che si limitano a sventolare nel [[motifs/wind|vento]]?  


Poeta:  
Non vedo e non sento soltanto strisce di stoffa;  
Ascolto il rumore delle armate in movimento, le voci delle sentinelle che gridano l’allarme,  
Le grida di giubilo di milioni di uomini. Ascolto la voce della Libertà!  
Sento i tamburi rullare, le trombe suonare;  
Io stesso mi muovo veloce, come se volassi.
Uso le ali degli uccelli terrestri e marine,  
E guardo giù da grande altezza.
Non nego i preziosi frutti della pace: vedo città popolose, ricchezze inestimabili,  
Innumerevoli fattorie, contadini che lavorano nei campi.
Vedo macchinari al lavoro, edifici che sorgono ovunque.
Vedo treni che viaggiano veloci lungo le ferrovie.
Vedo negozi, magazzini a Boston, Baltimora, Charleston, New Orleans.
Lontano a ovest, vedo immense distese di colture. Mi soffermo un attimo.
Poi volo verso le foreste del Nord, le piantagioni del Sud, e ancora in California.
Ovunque guardo, vedo profitti innumerevoli, persone impegnate nel lavoro, salari guadagnati onestamente.
Vedo un’[[concepts/identity|identità]] nazionale formata da trentotto stati vasti e potenti, (e molti altri ne seguiranno, )  
Vedo fortezze sulle rive dei porti, navi che entrano e escono.
E sopra tutto, il mio piccolo stendardo, lungo e a forma di spada,  
Si alza velocemente, simbolo di guerra e sfida. E ora le corde lo sollevano ancora più in alto.
Il lato del mio stendardo è blu, adornato da stelle. Abbandono la pace, su tutto il mare e sulla terra.  

Stendardo e Bandiera:  
Ancora più forte, ancora più alto, ancora più potente, o poeta! Ancora più lontano, ancora più ampio si diffonda il nostro dominio.
Non lasciate che i nostri figli considerino noi soltanto ricchezza e pace;  
Noi possiamo essere terrore e distruzione. E proprio così siamo ora.  
Non siamo più nessuno di quegli Stati vasti e arroganti, (né cinque, né dieci, )  
Né mercato, né deposito, né banca d’oro in questa città.
Ma tutto ciò – e la terra bruna che si estende ovunque, le miniere sottostanti – appartiene a noi.  
Anche le rive del mare, i fiumi grandi e piccoli, i campi che essi irrigano, i raccolti e i frutti. Tutto ciò è nostro.  
Le baie, i canali, le navi che vi navigano. Anche tutto questo appartiene a noi.  
Noi, su tutta questa vasta area – quei tre o quattro milioni di miglia quadrate, le capitali, quei quaranta milioni di persone. Oh poeta! Per tutta la vita, e anche dopo la morte, noi saremo i dominatori supremi.  
Da ora in poi, per mille anni, attraverso le tue parole, canteremo questa canzone all’anima di ogni bambino povero.  

[[archetypes/child|Bambino]]:  
Oh mio padre, non mi piacciono le case;  
Non significano nulla per me, né mi interessano i soldi.
Ma vorrei salire lassù, oh caro padre. Quel stendardo. Lo desidero ardentemente;  
Vorrei essere quel pennone. Devo esserlo.  

Padre:  
Mio [[archetypes/child|bambino]], mi riempi di angoscia.
Essere quel “stendardo” significherebbe affrontare troppi pericoli.
Piccolo mio, non sai cosa significhi oggi. E dopo oggi, per sempre.
Significherà non ottenere nulla, ma solo rischiare e sfidare tutto.
Andare incontro a guerre. Oh, quali guerre. Cosa c’entri tu con esse?  
Con passioni demoniache, massacri, morte prematura.  

Insegna:  
Demoni e [[motifs/death|morte]], allora io canto;  
Dò tutto ciò che ho, sì, tutto ciò che possiedo, per creare una bandiera a forma di _spada da utilizzare in guerra.  
Un piacere nuovo ed estatico, il chiacchiericcio gioioso dei bambini.
Tutto questo si fonde con i suoni della terra pacifica e con il rumore delle onde del mare;  
Ci sono navi nere che combattono tra le fumate.
Il freddo gelido del lontano nord, con i cedri e i pini che frusciano al vento.
Il rimbombo dei tamburi, il suono degli soldati che marciano. Il sole caldo che splende a sud.
Le onde che si infrangono sulla spiaggia, sia sulla mia costa orientale che su quella occidentale.
E tutto ciò che c’è tra queste due coste. Il mio fiume Mississippi, con i suoi meandri e le sue cascate.
I campi dell’Illinois, del Kansas, del Missouri.
Il Continente intero, che dona tutta la sua [[concepts/identity|IDENTITÀ]] senza riservarsi nulla.
Versatelo tutto! Inondate ciò che chiede, ciò che canta. Con tutto ciò che ho.
Fondete, unite, reclamate. Divorate tutto.
Niente più baci teneri, né suoni musicali delle labbra.
Ma solo l’[[motifs/night|NOTTE]] che emerge per sempre. Una voce ormai priva di persuasione.
Un grido simile a quello dei corvi, nel [[motifs/wind|VENTO]].  

Poeta:  
Le mie membra si dilatano, le mie vene pulsano; finalmente il mio tema è chiaro.  
Stenditi ampio come una bandiera che emerge dalla notte. Ti canto, orgoglioso e risoluto.  
Ho infranto ogni barriera che mi ostacolava, per troppo tempo sono stato sordo e cieco.
La mia udizione e la mia lingua mi sono tornate. Un piccolo bambino me le ha insegnate.  
Ascolto da lassù il tuo richiamo ironico. Insensati! Eppure ti canto comunque, o bandiera di guerra!  
Non sei certo simbolo di pace, né rappresenti la prosperità delle persone. Se necessario, distruggerai tutte quelle case preziose, piene di conforto e costruite con denaro.
Che possano rimanere intatte? Non per un’ora. Tu solo esisti sopra di loro.
O bandiera, non sei certo il denaro né i prodotti agricoli, né i beni materiali. Né le scorte preziose, né le merci trasportate dalle navi. Né le imbarcazioni potenti che portano e prendono carichi. Né la macchinaria, i veicoli, il commercio, i ricavi. Ma tu. Come ora ti vedo.
Emergere dalla notte, con il tuo fascio di stelle. Stelle sempre più grandi. Tu che dividi l’alba, tagli l’aria, toccato dal sole, misuri il cielo.
Amato da un povero bambino. Mentre gli altri sono impegnati o chiacchierano. Insegnando sempre la parsimonia.
O tu, lassù. Bandiera che ondeggia come un serpente che sibila in modo così misterioso. Irraggiungibile. Solo un’idea. Ma per cui si combatte con furia, rischiando la morte. Amato da me. Così amato. O bandiera che guida il giorno, portando le stelle dalla notte.
Inutile oggetto degli sguardi. Che tutto domini e pretenda. Proprietario assoluto di tutto. O bandiera e stendardo.
Anch’io lascio da parte il resto. Per quanto grande sia. Non è nulla. Case, macchinari. Non li vedo nemmeno. Vedo solo te. O bandiera guerriera! O ampio stendardo, con le tue strisce. Ti canto solo io. Sventolando lassù nel vento.



<span class="atom-split" data-atom="170_not_youth_pertains_to_me" data-title="Not Youth Pertains to Me" data-chapter="" data-kind="intro" data-tags="motif/music_and_song,concept/war_and_its_cost,concept/comradeship,concept/identity,form/lyric,cluster/identity,cluster/free_verse,cluster/eternal_return"></span>


_Cluster: DRUM-TAPS_  


Not youth pertains to me,  
Nor delicatesse, I cannot beguile the [[motifs/time|time]] with talk,  
Awkward in the parlor, neither a dancer nor elegant,  
In the learn’d coterie sitting constrain’d and still, for learning  
inures not to me,  
[[concepts/beauty|Beauty]], knowledge, inure not to me--yet there are two or three things  
inure to me,  
I have nourish’d the wounded and sooth’d many a dying soldier,  
And at intervals waiting or in the midst of camp,  
Composed these songs.  






<span class="qlang-split" data-lang="it"></span>

_Cluster: DRUM-TAPS_  


Né la giovinezza né le delicatezze mi appartengono;  
non posso ingannare il [[motifs/time|tempo]] con parole;  
impacciato nella sala da pranzo, né ballerino né elegante.
Nella compagnia degli studiosi resto seduto in silenzio, poiché lo studio  
non mi è di alcun aiuto.  
[[concepts/beauty|Bellezza]], conoscenza, nulla di tutto ciò mi appartiene.
Tuttavia, ci sono ancora due o tre cose che non riesco ad accettare.
Ho curato i feriti e consolato molti soldati morenti;  
in momenti di attesa, o nel bel mezzo del campo,  
ho composto queste canzoni.



<span class="atom-split" data-atom="172_world_take_good_notice" data-title="World Take Good Notice" data-chapter="" data-kind="intro" data-tags="motif/flags_banners,motif/stars,motif/white_and_red,concept/nationalism_and_patriotism,concept/the_union,cluster/identity,cluster/free_verse,cluster/eternal_return"></span>


_Cluster: DRUM-TAPS_  


World take good notice, silver [[motifs/stars|stars]] fading,  
Milky hue ript, wet of white detaching,  
Coals thirty-eight, baleful and burning,  
Scarlet, significant, hands off warning,  
Now and henceforth flaunt from these shores.  






<span class="qlang-split" data-lang="it"></span>

_Cluster: DRUM-TAPS_  


Il mondo prenda atto: le argentee [[motifs/stars|stelle]] stanno svanendo;  
Il colore latteo del cielo si squarcia, il bianco si stacca e cade;  
Trentotto carboni ardenti e minacciosi.
Il rosso scarlatto è un segnale d’allarme: tenetevi lontani.  
Da ora in poi, tutto questo verrà mostrato chiaramente da queste rive.



<span class="atom-split" data-atom="189_by_blue_ontario_s_shore" data-title="By Blue Ontario’s Shore" data-chapter="" data-kind="intro" data-tags="topos/poet_as_prophet,archetype/prophet,concept/democracy,concept/nationalism_and_patriotism,concept/the_self,concept/war_and_its_cost,concept/the_artist_and_society,concept/liberty,form/ode,form/free_verse,histref/american_civil_war,character/abraham_lincoln,cluster/identity,cluster/free_verse,cluster/eternal_return"></span>



By blue Ontario’s shore,  
As I mused of these warlike days and of peace return’d, and the  
dead that return no more,  
A [[characters/phantom|Phantom]] gigantic superb, with stern visage accosted me,  
Chant me the poem, it said, that comes from the [[motifs/soul|soul]] of [[settings/america|America]],  
chant me the carol of victory,  
And strike up the marches of Libertad, marches more powerful yet,  
And sing me before you go the song of the throes of [[concepts/democracy|Democracy]].  

([[concepts/democracy|Democracy]], the destin’d conqueror, yet treacherous lip-smiles everywhere,  
And [[motifs/death|death]] and infidelity at every step.)  

2  
A Nation announcing itself,  
I myself make the only growth by which I can be appreciated,  
I reject none, accept all, then reproduce all in my own forms.  

A breed whose proof is in [[motifs/time|time]] and deeds,  
What we are we are, nativity is answer enough to objections,  
We wield ourselves as a weapon is wielded,  
We are powerful and tremendous in ourselves,  
We are executive in ourselves, we are sufficient in the variety of  
ourselves,  
We are the most beautiful to ourselves and in ourselves,  
We stand [[concepts/self|self]]-pois’d in the middle, branching thence over the world,  
From Missouri, Nebraska, or Kansas, laughing attacks to scorn.  

Nothing is sinful to us outside of ourselves,  
Whatever appears, whatever does not appear, we are beautiful or  
sinful in ourselves only.  

(O Mother--O Sisters dear!  
If we are lost, no victor else has destroy’d us,  
It is by ourselves we go down to eternal [[motifs/night|night]].)  

3  
Have you thought there could be but a single supreme?  
There can be any number of supremes--one does not countervail  
another any more than one eyesight countervails another, or  
one life countervails another.  

All is eligible to all,  
All is for individuals, all is for you,  
No condition is prohibited, not God’s or any.  

All comes by the [[concepts/body|body]], only health puts you rapport with the universe.  

Produce great Persons, the rest follows.  

4  
Piety and conformity to them that like,  
Peace, obesity, allegiance, to them that like,  
I am he who tauntingly compels men, women, nations,  
Crying, Leap from your seats and contend for your lives!  

I am he who walks the States with a barb’d tongue, questioning every  
one I meet,  
Who are you that wanted only to be told what you knew before?  
Who are you that wanted only a book to join you in your nonsense?  

(With pangs and cries as thine own O bearer of many children,  
These clamors wild to a race of [[concepts/pride|pride]] I give.)  

O lands, would you be freer than all that has ever been before?  
If you would be freer than all that has been before, come listen to me.  

[[concepts/fear|Fear]] grace, elegance, civilization, delicatesse,  
[[concepts/fear|Fear]] the mellow sweet, the sucking of honey--juice,  
Beware the advancing mortal ripening of [[concepts/nature|Nature]],  
Beware what precedes the [[motifs/decay|decay]] of the ruggedness of states and men.  

5  
Ages, precedents, have long been accumulating undirected materials,  
[[settings/america|America]] brings builders, and brings its own styles.  

The immortal poets of Asia and Europe have done their work and  
pass’d to other spheres,  
A work remains, the work of surpassing all they have done.  

[[settings/america|America]], curious toward foreign characters, stands by its own at all  
hazards,  
Stands removed, spacious, composite, sound, initiates the true use  
of precedents,  
Does not repel them or the past or what they have produced under  
their forms,  
Takes the lesson with calmness, perceives the corpse slowly borne  
from the house,  
Perceives that it waits a little while in the door, that it was  
fittest for its days,  
That its life has descended to the stalwart and [[motifs/well|well]]-shaped heir who  
approaches,  
And that he shall be fittest for his days.  

Any period one nation must lead,  
One land must be the promise and reliance of the future.  

These States are the amplest poem,  
Here is not merely a nation but a teeming Nation of nations,  
Here the doings of men correspond with the broadcast doings of the  
day and [[motifs/night|night]],  
Here is what moves in magnificent masses careless of particulars,  
Here are the roughs, beards, friendliness, combativeness, the [[motifs/soul|soul]] loves,  
Here the flowing trains, here the crowds, equality, diversity, the  
[[motifs/soul|soul]] loves.  

6  
Land of lands and bards to corroborate!  
Of them standing among them, one lifts to the light a west-bred face,  
To him the hereditary countenance bequeath’d both mother’s and father’s,  
His first parts substances, earth, [[motifs/water|water]], [[motifs/animals|animals]], trees,  
Built of the common stock, having room for far and near,  
Used to dispense with other lands, incarnating this land,  
Attracting it [[concepts/body|body]] and [[motifs/soul|soul]] to himself, hanging on its neck with  
incomparable [[motifs/love|love]],  
Plunging his seminal muscle into its merits and demerits,  
Making its cities, beginnings, events, diversities, wars, vocal in him,  
Making its rivers, lakes, bays, embouchure in him,  
Mississippi with yearly freshets and changing chutes, Columbia,  
Niagara, Hudson, spending themselves lovingly in him,  
If the Atlantic coast stretch or the Pacific coast stretch, he  
stretching with them North or South,  
Spanning between them East and West, and touching whatever is between them,  
Growths growing from him to offset the growths of pine, cedar, hemlock,  
live-oak, locust, chestnut, hickory, cottonwood, orange, magnolia,  
Tangles as tangled in him as any canebrake or swamp,  
He likening sides and peaks of [[settings/mountains|mountains]], forests coated with  
northern transparent ice,  
Off him pasturage sweet and natural as savanna, upland, prairie,  
Through him flights, whirls, screams, answering those of the  
fish-hawk, mocking-bird, [[motifs/night|night]]-heron, and eagle,  
His spirit surrounding his country’s spirit, unclosed to good and evil,  
Surrounding the essences of real things, old times and present times,  
Surrounding just found shores, islands, tribes of red aborigines,  
Weather-beaten vessels, landings, settlements, embryo stature and muscle,  
The haughty defiance of the Year One, war, peace, the formation of  
the Constitution,  
The separate States, the simple elastic scheme, the immigrants,  
The [[concepts/union|Union]] always swarming with blatherers and always sure and impregnable,  
The unsurvey’d interior, log-houses, clearings, wild [[motifs/animals|animals]],  
hunters, trappers,  
Surrounding the multiform agriculture, mines, temperature, the  
gestation of new States,  
Congress convening every Twelfth-month, the members duly coming  
up from the uttermost parts,  
Surrounding the noble character of mechanics and farmers, especially  
the young men,  
Responding their manners, speech, dress, friendships, the gait they  
have of persons who never knew how it felt to stand in the  
presence of superiors,  
The freshness and candor of their physiognomy, the copiousness and  
decision of their phrenology,  
The picturesque looseness of their carriage, their fierceness when wrong’d,  
The fluency of their speech, their delight in music, their curiosity,  
good temper and open-handedness, the whole composite make,  
The prevailing ardor and enterprise, the large amativeness,  
The perfect equality of the female with the male, the fluid movement  
of the population,  
The superior marine, free commerce, fisheries, whaling, gold-digging,  
Wharf-hemm’d cities, railroad and steamboat lines intersecting all points,  
Factories, mercantile life, labor-saving machinery, the Northeast,  
Northwest, Southwest,  
[[settings/manhattan|Manhattan]] firemen, the Yankee swap, southern plantation life,  
[[concepts/slavery|Slavery]]--the murderous, treacherous conspiracy to raise it upon the  
ruins of all the rest,  
On and on to the grapple with it--Assassin! then your life or ours  
be the stake, and respite no more.  

7  
(Lo, high toward heaven, this day,  
Libertad, from the conqueress’ field return’d,  
I mark the new aureola around your head,  
No more of soft astral, but dazzling and fierce,  
With war’s flames and the lambent lightnings playing,  
And your port immovable where you stand,  
With still the inextinguishable glance and the clinch’d and lifted fist,  
And your foot on the neck of the menacing one, the scorner utterly  
crush’d beneath you,  
The menacing arrogant one that strode and advanced with his  
senseless scorn, bearing the murderous knife,  
The wide-swelling one, the braggart that would yesterday do so much,  
To-day a carrion dead and damn’d, the despised of all the earth,  
An offal rank, to the dunghill maggots spurn’d.)  

8  
Others take finish, but the Republic is ever constructive and ever  
keeps vista,  
Others adorn the past, but you O days of the present, I adorn you,  
O days of the future I believe in you--I isolate myself for your sake,  
O [[settings/america|America]] because you build for mankind I build for you,  
O [[motifs/well|well]]-beloved stone-cutters, I lead them who plan with decision  
and science,  
Lead the present with friendly hand toward the future.  
(Bravas to all impulses sending sane children to the next age!  
But damn that which spends itself with no thought of the stain,  
pains, dismay, feebleness, it is bequeathing.)  

9  
I listened to the [[characters/phantom|Phantom]] by Ontario’s shore,  
I heard the voice arising demanding bards,  
By them all native and grand, by them alone can these States be  
fused into the compact organism of a Nation.  

To hold men together by paper and seal or by compulsion is no account,  
That only holds men together which aggregates all in a living principle,  
as the hold of the limbs of the [[concepts/body|body]] or the fibres of plants.  

Of all races and eras these States with veins full of poetical stuff most  
need poets, and are to have the greatest, and use them the greatest,  
Their Presidents shall not be their common referee so much as their  
poets shall.  

([[motifs/soul|Soul]] of [[motifs/love|love]] and tongue of [[motifs/fire|fire]]!  
Eye to pierce the deepest deeps and sweep the world!  
Ah Mother, prolific and full in all besides, yet how long barren, barren?)  

10  
Of these States the poet is the equable man,  
Not in him but off from him things are [[concepts/grotesque|grotesque]], eccentric, fail of  
their full returns,  
Nothing out of its place is good, nothing in its place is bad,  
He bestows on every object or quality its fit proportion, neither  
more nor less,  
He is the arbiter of the diverse, he is the key,  
He is the equalizer of his age and land,  
He supplies what wants supplying, he checks what wants checking,  
In peace out of him speaks the spirit of peace, large, rich,  
thrifty, building populous towns, encouraging agriculture, arts,  
commerce, lighting the study of man, the [[motifs/soul|soul]], health,  
[[concepts/immortality|immortality]], government,  
In war he is the best backer of the war, he fetches artillery as  
good as the engineer’s, he can make every word he speaks draw [[motifs/blood|blood]],  
The years straying toward infidelity he withholds by his steady [[concepts/faith|faith]],  
He is no arguer, he is judgment, ([[concepts/nature|Nature]] accepts him absolutely,)  
He judges not as the judge judges but as the sun failing round  
helpless thing,  
As he sees the farthest he has the most [[concepts/faith|faith]],  
His thoughts are the hymns of the praise of things,  
In the dispute on God and [[concepts/eternity|eternity]] he is silent,  
He sees [[concepts/eternity|eternity]] less like a play with a prologue and denouement,  
He sees [[concepts/eternity|eternity]] in men and women, he does not see men and women  
as [[motifs/dreams|dreams]] or dots.  

For the great Idea, the idea of perfect and free individuals,  
For that, the bard walks in advance, leader of leaders,  
The attitude of him cheers up slaves and horrifies foreign despots.  

Without extinction is Liberty, without retrograde is Equality,  
They live in the feelings of young men and the best women,  
(Not for nothing have the indomitable heads of the earth been always  
ready to fall for Liberty.)  

11  
For the great Idea,  
That, O my brethren, that is the mission of poets.  

Songs of stern defiance ever ready,  
Songs of the rapid arming and the march,  
The [[motifs/flag|flag]] of peace quick-folded, and instead the [[motifs/flag|flag]] we know,  
Warlike [[motifs/flag|flag]] of the great Idea.  

(Angry cloth I saw there leaping!  
I stand again in leaden rain your flapping folds saluting,  
I sing you over all, flying beckoning through the fight--O the  
hard-contested fight!  
The cannons ope their rosy-flashing muzzles--the hurtled balls scream,  
The battle-front forms amid the smoke--the volleys pour incessant  
from the line,  
Hark, the ringing word Charge!--now the tussle and the furious  
maddening yells,  
Now the corpses tumble curl’d upon the ground,  
Cold, cold in [[motifs/death|death]], for precious life of you,  
Angry cloth I saw there leaping.)  

12  
Are you he who would assume a place to teach or be a poet here in  
the States?  
The place is august, the terms obdurate.  

Who would assume to teach here may [[motifs/well|well]] prepare himself [[concepts/body|body]] and mind,  
He may [[motifs/well|well]] survey, ponder, arm, fortify, harden, make lithe himself,  
He shall surely be question’d beforehand by me with many and stern questions.  

Who are you indeed who would talk or sing to [[settings/america|America]]?  
Have you studied out the land, its idioms and men?  
Have you learn’d the physiology, phrenology, politics, geography,  
[[concepts/pride|pride]], [[concepts/freedom|freedom]], [[concepts/friendship|friendship]] of the land? its substratums and objects?  
Have you consider’d the organic compact of the first day of the  
first year of Independence, sign’d by the Commissioners, ratified  
by the States, and read by Washington at the head of the army?  
Have you possess’d yourself of the Federal Constitution?  
Do you see who have left all feudal processes and poems behind them,  
and assumed the poems and processes of [[concepts/democracy|Democracy]]?  
Are you faithful to things? do you teach what the land and sea, the  
bodies of men, womanhood, amativeness, heroic angers, teach?  
Have you sped through fleeting customs, popularities?  
Can you hold your hand against all seductions, follies, whirls,  
fierce contentions? are you very strong? are you really of the  
whole People?  
Are you not of some coterie? some school or mere religion?  
Are you done with reviews and criticisms of life? animating now to  
life itself?  
Have you vivified yourself from the maternity of these States?  
Have you too the old ever-fresh forbearance and impartiality?  
Do you hold the like [[motifs/love|love]] for those hardening to maturity? for the  
last-born? little and big? and for the errant?  

What is this you bring my [[settings/america|America]]?  
Is it uniform with my country?  
Is it not something that has been better told or done before?  
Have you not imported this or the spirit of it in some [[motifs/ship|ship]]?  
Is it not a mere tale? a rhyme? a prettiness?--Is the good old cause in it?  
Has it not dangled long at the heels of the poets, politicians,  
literats, of enemies’ lands?  
Does it not assume that what is notoriously gone is still here?  
Does it answer universal needs? will it improve manners?  
Does it sound with trumpet-voice the proud victory of the [[concepts/union|Union]] in  
that secession war?  
Can your performance face the open fields and the seaside?  
Will it absorb into me as I absorb food, air, to appear again in my  
strength, gait, face?  
Have real employments contributed to it? original makers, not mere  
amanuenses?  
Does it meet modern discoveries, calibres, facts, face to face?  
What does it mean to American persons, progresses, cities? Chicago,  
Kanada, Arkansas?  
Does it see behind the apparent custodians the real custodians  
standing, menacing, silent, the mechanics, Manhattanese, Western  
men, Southerners, significant alike in their apathy, and in the  
promptness of their [[motifs/love|love]]?  
Does it see what finally befalls, and has always finally befallen,  
each temporizer, patcher, [[archetypes/outsider|outsider]], partialist, alarmist,  
infidel, who has ever ask’d any thing of [[settings/america|America]]?  
What mocking and scornful negligence?  
The track strew’d with the [[motifs/dust|dust]] of skeletons,  
By the roadside others disdainfully toss’d.  

13  
Rhymes and rhymers pass away, poems distill’d from poems pass away,  
The swarms of reflectors and the polite pass, and leave ashes,  
Admirers, importers, obedient persons, make but the soil of literature,  
[[settings/america|America]] justifies itself, give it [[motifs/time|time]], no disguise can deceive it  
or conceal from it, it is impassive enough,  
Only toward the likes of itself will it advance to meet them,  
If its poets appear it will in due [[motifs/time|time]] advance to meet them, there  
is no [[concepts/fear|fear]] of mistake,  
(The proof of a poet shall be sternly deferr’d till his country  
absorbs him as affectionately as he has absorb’d it.)  

He masters whose spirit masters, he tastes sweetest who results  
sweetest in the long run,  
The [[motifs/blood|blood]] of the brawn beloved of [[motifs/time|time]] is unconstraint;  
In the need of songs, philosophy, an appropriate native grand-opera,  
shipcraft, any craft,  
He or she is greatest who contributes the greatest original  
practical example.  

Already a nonchalant breed, silently emerging, appears on the streets,  
People’s lips salute only doers, lovers, satisfiers, positive knowers,  
There will shortly be no more priests, I say their work is done,  
[[motifs/death|Death]] is without emergencies here, but life is perpetual emergencies here,  
Are your [[concepts/body|body]], days, manners, superb? after [[motifs/death|death]] you shall be superb,  
Justice, health, [[concepts/self|self]]-esteem, clear the way with irresistible power;  
How dare you place any thing before a man?  

14  
Fall behind me States!  
A man before all--myself, typical, before all.  

Give me the pay I have served for,  
Give me to sing the songs of the great Idea, take all the rest,  
I have loved the earth, sun, [[motifs/animals|animals]], I have despised riches,  
I have given aims to every one that ask’d, stood up for the stupid  
and crazy, devoted my income and labor to others,  
Hated tyrants, argued not concerning God, had patience and indulgence  
toward the people, taken off my hat to nothing known or unknown,  
Gone freely with powerful uneducated persons and with the young,  
and with the mothers of families,  
Read these [[motifs/leaves|leaves]] to myself in the open air, tried them by trees,  
[[motifs/stars|stars]], rivers,  
Dismiss’d whatever insulted my own [[motifs/soul|soul]] or defiled my [[concepts/body|body]],  
Claim’d nothing to myself which I have not carefully claim’d for  
others on the same terms,  
Sped to the camps, and comrades found and accepted from every State,  
(Upon this breast has many a dying soldier lean’d to breathe his last,  
This arm, this hand, this voice, have nourish’d, rais’d, restored,  
To life recalling many a prostrate form;)  
I am willing to wait to be understood by the growth of the taste of myself,  
Rejecting none, permitting all.  

(Say O Mother, have I not to your thought been faithful?  
Have I not through life kept you and yours before me?)  

15  
I swear I begin to see the meaning of these things,  
It is not the earth, it is not [[settings/america|America]] who is so great,  
It is I who am great or to be great, it is You up there, or any one,  
It is to walk rapidly through civilizations, governments, theories,  
Through poems, pageants, shows, to form individuals.  

Underneath all, individuals,  
I swear nothing is good to me now that ignores individuals,  
The American compact is altogether with individuals,  
The only government is that which makes minute of individuals,  
The whole theory of the universe is directed unerringly to one  
single individual--namely to You.  

(Mother! with subtle sense severe, with the naked sword in your hand,  
I saw you at last refuse to treat but directly with individuals.)  

16  
Underneath all, Nativity,  
I swear I will stand by my own nativity, pious or impious so be it;  
I swear I am charm’d with nothing except nativity,  
Men, women, cities, nations, are only beautiful from nativity.  

Underneath all is the Expression of [[motifs/love|love]] for men and women,  
(I swear I have seen enough of mean and impotent modes of expressing  
[[motifs/love|love]] for men and women,  
After this day I take my own modes of expressing [[motifs/love|love]] for men and  
women.) in myself,  

I swear I will have each quality of my race in myself,  
(Talk as you like, he only suits these States whose manners favor  
the audacity and [[concepts/sublime|sublime]] turbulence of the States.)  

Underneath the lessons of things, spirits, [[concepts/nature|Nature]], governments,  
ownerships, I swear I perceive other lessons,  
Underneath all to me is myself, to you yourself, (the same  
monotonous old song.)  

17  
O I see flashing that this [[settings/america|America]] is only you and me,  
Its power, weapons, testimony, are you and me,  
Its crimes, lies, thefts, defections, are you and me,  
Its Congress is you and me, the officers, capitols, armies, ships,  
are you and me,  
Its endless gestations of new States are you and me,  
The war, (that war so bloody and grim, the war I will henceforth  
forget), was you and me,  
Natural and artificial are you and me,  
[[concepts/freedom|Freedom]], language, poems, employments, are you and me,  
Past, present, future, are you and me.  

I dare not shirk any part of myself,  
Not any part of [[settings/america|America]] good or bad,  
Not to build for that which builds for mankind,  
Not to balance ranks, complexions, creeds, and the sexes,  
Not to justify science nor the march of equality,  
Nor to feed the arrogant [[motifs/blood|blood]] of the brawn belov’d of [[motifs/time|time]].  

I am for those that have never been master’d,  
For men and women whose tempers have never been master’d,  
For those whom laws, theories, conventions, can never master.  

I am for those who walk abreast with the whole earth,  
Who inaugurate one to inaugurate all.  

I will not be outfaced by irrational things,  
I will penetrate what it is in them that is sarcastic upon me,  
I will make cities and civilizations defer to me,  
This is what I have learnt from [[settings/america|America]]--it is the amount, and it I  
teach again.  

([[concepts/democracy|Democracy]], while weapons were everywhere aim’d at your breast,  
I saw you serenely give birth to immortal children, saw in [[motifs/dreams|dreams]]  
your dilating form,  
Saw you with spreading mantle covering the world.)  

18  
I will confront these shows of the day and [[motifs/night|night]],  
I will know if I am to be less than they,  
I will see if I am not as majestic as they,  
I will see if I am not as subtle and real as they,  
I will see if I am to be less generous than they,  
I will see if I have no meaning, while the houses and ships have meaning,  
I will see if the fishes and [[motifs/birds|birds]] are to be enough for themselves,  
and I am not to be enough for myself.  

I match my spirit against yours you orbs, growths, [[settings/mountains|mountains]], brutes,  
Copious as you are I absorb you all in myself, and become the master myself,  
[[settings/america|America]] isolated yet embodying all, what is it finally except myself?  
These States, what are they except myself?  

I know now why the earth is gross, tantalizing, wicked, it is for my sake,  
I take you specially to be mine, you terrible, rude forms.  


(Mother, bend down, bend close to me your face,  
I know not what these plots and wars and deferments are for,  
I know not fruition’s success, but I know that through war and crime  
your work goes on, and must yet go on.)  

19  
Thus by blue Ontario’s shore,  
While the winds fann’d me and the waves came trooping toward me,  
I thrill’d with the power’s pulsations, and the charm of my theme  
was upon me,  
Till the tissues that held me parted their ties upon me.  

And I saw the free souls of poets,  
The loftiest bards of past ages strode before me,  
Strange large men, long unwaked, undisclosed, were disclosed to me.  

20  
O my rapt verse, my call, mock me not!  
Not for the bards of the past, not to invoke them have I launch’d  
you forth,  
Not to call even those lofty bards here by Ontario’s shores,
